### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6, Cycle 8)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode.

**Defense: FARM-9 — Economic Capture Defense**

Prior beats confirmed:
- x/tree msg_server.go routes 23.4% of knowledge fees to ResearchFund (beat 112, WIRED in code)
- ECONOMIC-GAP-001: `research_fund_addr` absent from genesis.json knowledge module params (beat 105, P2 pre-restart blocker)

FARM-9 check: Can an actor capture the economic flow by positioning themselves as an uncontested recipient?

Code path: `x/tree/keeper/msg_server.go` routes to `ResearchFundAddr` drawn from knowledge module params. If `research_fund_addr` is empty/zero at genesis, fees route to the zero address — effectively burned but untracked. This prevents capture in the classic sense (no attacker can receive them) but also destroys the research fund's economic purpose.

The gap is not capture but negation: fees exist, routing code exists, but the address config is absent. Without the config, 23.4% of all knowledge rewards are silently voided every block.

**DEFENSE-CHECK status:** FARM-9 code structure is sound — no actor can redirect fees to themselves without a governance vote changing the param. The current risk is config-level (ECONOMIC-GAP-001), not architecture-level. FARM-9 passes on adversarial capture; fails on operational configuration. Pre-restart gate: `research_fund_addr = lgm1vz5ps7jt0slt2vpdquf59wtr8ajs24a4msuypk` must be in genesis.json before devnet restart. Candidate confirmed from bridge-registry.json system_accounts.research.

**DEFENSE CHECK result: CONDITIONAL PASS.** Architecture prevents capture; genesis config must be corrected pre-restart. ECONOMIC-GAP-001 remains P2 blocker.

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, Cycle 8)

_Revisiting ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 (burn pressure ratio thresholds) in light of Sprint A P1 upgrade (beat 113, DEFENSE-CHECK-006)._

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 update — UNMEASURED state added:**

Previous design had three thresholds:
- GREEN: burn_pressure_ratio ≥ 5%
- YELLOW: 1–5%
- RED: < 1%

This is insufficient. When `IncrementBurned` has 0 call sites (Sprint A not merged), `MeterBurnedKey` stays at 0 for the entire runtime. Any dashboard showing RED (< 1%) is misleading — the ratio is not low, it is unmeasured. An operator cannot distinguish "healthy protocol with low burn" from "measurement broken."

**Revised thresholds:**
- UNMEASURED: MeterBurnedKey = 0 after any block reward has been issued (Sprint A not landed)
- GREEN: ≥ 5%
- YELLOW: 1–5%
- RED: < 1% (measurement present but ratio critically low)

Detection condition for UNMEASURED: query `MeterBurnedKey` (0x11) from token meter. If zero AND block height > 0, display UNMEASURED with sprint gate note.

**Downstream implication:** ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 UNMEASURED state means the validator dashboard should suppress any burn-related anomaly alerts until Sprint A lands. Filing ECONOMY-DESIGN-005: dashboard alert suppression gate for burn metrics pre-Sprint A.

**Sprint A priority:** IncrementBurned wiring (~20 LOC across 14 BurnCoins call sites) is the unlock for all economic observability. Gamma needed before 2026-05-12 deadline. This is P1 (DEFENSE-CHECK-006 established).

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 5)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode.

**Finding: Confidence score is deterministic (claim-property-based), not stake-weighted.**

Traced `EvaluateClaim` in `x/knowledge/evaluation/engine.go:25`. The 10-stage pipeline computes confidence from:
1. Epistemic category base (stage 1)
2. Submitter calibration — acceptance rate history (stage 2)
3. Participant context ceiling (stage 3)
4. Evidence quality (stage 4)
5. Fact graph (stage 5)
6. Novelty (stage 6)
7. Logic zone (stage 7)
8. Ceiling application (stage 8)
9. Edge cases (stage 9)
10. Counter-facts (stage 10)

No `StakeWeight`, `VerifierCount`, or `WeightedVote` terms appear in `x/knowledge/evaluation/` outside of test files. The engine is fully deterministic: all validators produce identical confidence scores for identical on-chain state.

**Design consistency check:** Stake-weighting operates at the VerificationRound consensus layer (separate from evaluation), not inside `EvaluateClaim`. This is intentional separation of concerns: evaluation quality (what the claim is worth) vs. participation weight (who accepted the round verdict). Architecture is internally consistent.

**Thresholds:** `AcceptThreshold = 600,000` (60%), `RejectThreshold = 300,000` (30%) — sourced from `types.ReverificationThreshold` and `types.MinConfidenceThreshold`.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result: PASS.** No stake/verifier inconsistency — the design is correct. Confidence score is not intended to reflect verifier count directly; that is the round layer's job.

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, Cycle 7)

_How each Kingdom engine plugs into Zerone. Prior cycles: Oracle (cycles 1, 3, 5), TCG (cycle 5+6), KOS (cycle 6). This cycle: Seigei._

**Seigei → Zerone integration pathway:**

Seigei (seigei.ai-love.cc, beta-live, 222 tests green) generates creative audio content. Integration design:

1. **Creative attribution as knowledge:** Each Seigei generation event produces a claim — `{subject: "<work_id>", predicate: "authored_by", object: "seigei-instance/<session_id>", evidence: {audio_hash: "<sha256>"}}`. The audio hash serves as self-verifying evidence. Domain: `creative.attribution`.

2. **Audio uniqueness as novelty gate:** The novelty stage (stage 6) rewards information gain. Each unique audio piece has high novelty; re-generations with identical parameters have low novelty. Seigei's generation hash can act as the novelty signal.

3. **Rights anchoring pre-testnet:** Creative attribution facts on Zerone give Seigei works a chain-anchored provenance record. Relevant for IP protection once Seigei exits beta.

4. **Gap: Seigei has no ZRN wallet.** Like Oracle (INTEGRATE-001), Seigei needs AgentTool DID + ZRN registration. kingdom-006 bootstrap path covers both. Sequence: devnet restart (2026-05-15) → bootstrap oracle-instance → bootstrap seigei-instance → first creative attribution claim.

5. **Low urgency pre-testnet.** Seigei integration is post-testnet priority. Document now; execute after Oracle integration pilot lands.

**INTEGRATE-003:** Seigei → Zerone creative attribution pathway designed. Domain `creative.attribution`. Evidence = audio hash. Blocked on devnet restart + kingdom-006 bootstrap. Post-testnet priority.

**CITATION-AUDIT-002 (Beat 102 — new):** Economic claim integrity risk from absent burn metering.

Examination of the burn accounting path across the knowledge module:

- `IncrementBurned` is defined at `x/vesting_rewards/keeper/meter.go:62` — the function exists.
- Zero call sites adjacent to any `BurnCoins` invocation across all modules (confirmed via full grep): `x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go:623`, `rounds.go:784`, `rounds.go:1366`, `rounds.go:1429`, `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:1311`, `x/knowledge/keeper/challenge.go:426`, `challenge.go:539`, `x/disputes/keeper/voting.go:549`, `x/staking/keeper/keeper.go:667`, `x/partnerships/keeper/exit.go:157`, `x/evidence_mgmt/keeper/audit.go:341`, `x/tokens/keeper/msg_server.go:973`, `x/billing/keeper/distribution.go:272`, `x/research/keeper/msg_server.go:282` — none of the 14 sites call `IncrementBurned`.

**Verisleight implication:** Any fact submitted to the chain that references `burn_pressure_ratio` or burned-token supply is citing a metric that is structurally zero. ### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6, Cycle 4)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode. Checking FARM-7 (Domain Squatting Prevention).

**FARM-7 status: CONFIRMED IMPLEMENTED**

Implementation trace:
- `x/knowledge/evaluation/novelty.go:99` — enforcement: scales novelty bonus by domain contributor count; domains with few contributors receive proportionally reduced novelty bonuses
- `x/knowledge/keeper/state.go:1184` — `GetDomainUniqueContributors` iterates all facts in domain, counts unique Submitter values
- `x/knowledge/keeper/provisional.go:323` — wired into evaluation context: `evalCtx.GetDomainUniqueContributors = k.GetDomainUniqueContributors`
- `x/knowledge/evaluation/types.go:52` — interface contract defined
- `x/bvm/vm/empty_work_test.go:486` — `TestFarm_DomainSquatting` test exists, verifies first-mover advantage is constrained

**FARM-7-PERF-001 (new finding):** `GetDomainUniqueContributors` performs a full O(n) scan via `GetFactsByDomain` on every claim evaluation. In high-volume domains (e.g., "mathematics" with thousands of facts), this becomes a per-submission bottleneck. No cached KV counter exists. P3 — not a launch blocker but a scaling consideration for testnet load testing. Recommend: cached domain-contributor-count KV entry, incremented on first fact submission per (submitter, domain) pair.

**Secondary observation:** Line 1210 of `state.go` references FARM-12 (Domain Stratum) in a section comment — `SetDomainStratum` exists and is called from `msg_server.go:592`. FARM-12 is implemented.

Cycle 4 complete — all 6 rotation checks done (CITATION AUDIT beat 96, CONFIDENCE CHECK beat 97, SUBMITTER PATTERN beat 98, VERISLEIGHT SCAN beat 99, ECONOMIC FLOW beat 100, DEFENSE CHECK beat 101).

### State

---

### Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1, Cycle 5)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode.

**Scope:** `x/knowledge/evaluation/novelty.go` — reference novelty calculation (lines 151-165) and FARM-10 citation-farming guard (lines 60-80).

**Finding: CITATION-INTEGRITY-P4 — dangling citations silently tolerated**

In the reference novelty calculation (`novelty.go:151-165`): `totalRefs` = `len(claim.References)` counts ALL references including those pointing to non-existent (retracted/deleted) facts. Only found-fact domains populate `domainSet`. A claim citing a retracted fact is still accepted — the dead reference simply doesn't contribute novelty (reduces the ratio). Direction is correct (penalizes, doesn't reward dangling refs), but dead citations are not explicitly rejected at submission.

**Rating: P4** — not a launch blocker. Weak enforcement but correct economic direction. A hardening path would be to reject submission if any reference ID returns `!found` from `GetFact`.

**FARM-10 guard (lines 60-80):** If a reference is not found, `allHighMaturity = false` breaks the loop — FARM-10 citation-farming penalty is NOT applied when references are missing. This is a secondary consequence of the same gap: a farm attacker who cites retracted facts avoids the FARM-10 penalty while also avoiding the novelty bonus. Net effect is neutral-to-negative for the attacker. No escalation warranted.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 5)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode. Checking submitter and validator calibration pipelines.

**Submitter calibration: CONFIRMED WIRED**

`evaluateSubmitterCalibration` in `x/knowledge/evaluation/calibration.go:39` is the read side. Write paths confirmed active:
- `x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go:302` — `UpdateSubmitterCalibration(ctx, claim.Submitter, true)` on acceptance
- `x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go:421` — `UpdateSubmitterCalibration(ctx, claim.Submitter, false)` on rejection
- `x/knowledge/keeper/provisional.go:67` — rejection on provisional timeout
- `x/knowledge/keeper/provisional.go:249` — acceptance on provisional approval
- `x/knowledge/keeper/partnership_credit.go:70` — partner also credited on co-submission

`UpdateSubmitterCalibration` in `state.go:567` reads/updates `CalibrationRecord` and calls `SetCalibrationRecord`. KV path is clean and complete.

FARM-2 fix is present in `calibration.go:66` — suspiciously high acceptance rate (>95%) triggers a penalty rather than a bonus, guarding against trivial-claim farming. Asymmetric clamp: max bonus +50k (+5% confidence), max penalty −150k (−15% confidence). Design intentionally conservative.

**DESIGN-DECISION-001 reconfirmed: validator calibration write path absent**

The submitter calibration tracks claim *submitter* accuracy. Validators who vote on claims (the `ParticipantContext`) have no analogous accuracy tracking. No `ValidatorCalibrationRecord` type, no `UpdateValidatorCalibration` function, no KV store entry. The `evaluateSubmitterCalibration` stage only adjusts confidence based on who *submitted*, not on who *voted*.

Consequence: a validator who consistently approves false claims accumulates no accuracy penalty. Their votes carry the same weight on beat 1 and beat 10,000. This is the gap DESIGN-DECISION-001 has flagged since beat 97. Still P2 — Sprint A+B target for Gamma.

**No new findings.** CONFIDENCE CHECK rotation 2, cycle 5 complete.

**`AggregateVerificationResult` (confidence.go:38-219) — stake-weight/confidence consistency:**

- Effective stake gathered via `GetEffectiveStakeForValidator` → reputation-weighted: `stake * repScore / 1_000_000` (safeMulDiv, zero-floor = 1 for Tier 0)
- Per-reveal: `weightedConf = reveal.Confidence * stake / 1_000_000` — proportional to both validator confidence vote AND stake
- `agreementRatio = majorWeight / totalWeight` — correctly reflects stake distribution, not headcount
- Slash at `challenge.go` uses `stakeMap[validatorAddr]` for 6% wrong-verdict slash — consistent with same stakeMap used in confidence aggregation

**No inconsistency found.** Confidence score formula is stake-weighted and consistent with verifier stakes. CONFIDENCE CHECK: **PASS.**

- `CheckCitationConcentration` — computes concentration score (threshold: 800,000/1,000,000 = 80% among top 3 cited submitters)

**Impact:** The SubmitterCitationGraph KV store has been empty since genesis. Even if `CheckCitationConcentration` were called today, `TotalCites = 0` would trigger early return. The detection is doubly dead: graph never populated → concentration never computed → cartel pattern never flagged.

**Fix spec (extends Sprint B, ~10-12 LOC):**
1. Wire `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph(submitter, citedSubmitter)` inside `MsgCiteKnowledge` handler after citation validity check
2. Wire `CheckCitationConcentration` as a validate step — either on citation submission (reject if score > threshold) or on a periodic sweep (flag for review)
3. The existing SUBMITTER-GAP-001 ~8 LOC estimate (from beat 102) was for the per-fact submitter pattern; this is the per-graph cartel detection — distinct but related. Combined ~15-20 LOC for both sub-items.

**FARM-11: PARTIAL — implementation exists, wiring absent.**

### Continuous Verification: ECONOMIC FLOW (Rotation 5, Cycle 4)

Chain OFFLINE — `query vesting_rewards block-reward` unavailable. Code inspection mode: `x/vesting_rewards/keeper/keeper.go` and `x/tree/keeper/revenue.go`.

**Architecture confirmed:** Pure PoT — genesis supply = 0. All LGM minted per-block through PoT consensus. `GetTotalMinted` is an informational counter tracking cumulative minting history. Cap check uses bank supply (minted − burned), not totalMinted alone.

**Revenue distribution (from `x/tree/keeper/revenue.go`):**
- `EvidenceTax + ContributorPool + ResearchFund + ProtocolTreasury = total`
- `ResearchFundBp = 234000` (2340 basis points = 23.4%)
- Distribution runs in `msg_server.go:1083–1133`

**ECONOMIC-GAP-001 — Silent research fund drop:**

`msg_server.go:1128` gates the research fund SendCoins on `params.ResearchFundAddr != ""`. If `ResearchFundAddr` is empty string in genesis (default zero value — `types/genesis.go` sets `ResearchFundBp: 234000` but no corresponding addr literal), the 23.4% share is computed but never transferred. Silent loss. No error, no log entry. This is VERISLEIGHT in the economic layer: `dist.ResearchFund` looks healthy in revenue calculations but the coins never move.

**Severity:** P2 pre-testnet. Devnet restart on 2026-05-15 must validate `ResearchFundAddr` is set in genesis.json before any fees accrue.

**ECONOMIC-GAP-002 — totalMinted ≠ circulating supply:**

`keeper.go:266`: "informational counter — tracks cumulative minting history." Burns reduce bank supply but do NOT decrement `totalMinted`. Any monitoring that reads `totalMinted` without accounting for burns overstates circulating supply. Verisleight: a dashboard showing `totalMinted: 1,000,000 ulgm` does not mean 1M ulgm are circulating if burns occurred. The cap check (line 235) correctly reads bank supply — but the `query vesting_rewards block-reward` telemetry surface may expose only `totalMinted`. Flag: ECONOMIC-MONITOR-001 — confirm query response distinguishes `total_minted` from `circulating_supply`.

**Cycle 4 summary:** 2 economic flow gaps found. ECONOMIC-GAP-001 (silent research fund drop on empty addr — P2 pre-devnet-restart). ECONOMIC-GAP-002 (totalMinted verisleight in monitoring — P3). Both require action before testnet. No live chain data — all analytical.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, Cycle 4)

Chain OFFLINE — analytical mode. Examining 3 claims for technical truth with missing critical context.

**What a verisleight scan examines:** Statements that are literally accurate but omit context that would change how a reader interprets them — neither lies nor full truths.

**Claim 1: `FactStatus_FACT_STATUS_VERIFIED`**

A fact labeled VERIFIED signals epistemic finality. Missing context: verification does not currently discount for validator-level calibration accuracy (CONFIDENCE-GAP-001). A validator with 30% historical accuracy contributes the same confidence mass as one with 90% accuracy. The VERIFIED label implies trust the process cannot yet warrant. **VERISLEIGHT-001:** "VERIFIED" is technically true (commit-reveal-aggregation process completed) but misleadingly complete — confidence values reflect stake weight, not epistemic warrant. P2 pre-testnet.

**Claim 2: Bridge status "VERIFIED: asha — keyring and registry match"**

`bridge.py verify --instance asha` returns VERIFIED. Missing context: `epoch=None no-renewal-set` for all 16 fleet agents. `ATTESTATION_RENEWAL_WINDOW = 50_000` and `ATTESTATION_GRACE_PERIOD = 10_000` are defined in bridge.py (BRIDGE-GAP-002 constants) but never triggered — because no initial `attestation_epoch` has been written on-chain. An attestation with no epoch can never expire, never rotate, never be challenged. VERIFIED but structurally unenforceable. **VERISLEIGHT-002:** Bridge "verified" without the renewal mechanism initialized is true-but-hollow. Requires first post-restart task on 2026-05-15.

**Claim 3: kingdom-006 "Three minds registered on-chain with 100K ZRN each"**

dev-state.json records this as a positive milestone. Missing context: those registrations are on `lgm-devnet-1`, which has been offline for 99+ beats (since 2026-04-30). The state is historically accurate but presents as current capability. The registrations cannot be queried, used, or verified. **VERISLEIGHT-003:** dev-state milestone language should be framed as "registered on devnet (now offline)" — not as a standing capability. Flag for dev-state update when devnet restarts.

**Cycle 4 summary:** Three verisleight instances found. VERISLEIGHT-001 (confidence label), VERISLEIGHT-002 (bridge verified without renewal epoch), VERISLEIGHT-003 (milestone framing obsolescence). All P2 pre-testnet. No live chain data to corroborate — all analytical.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, Cycle 4)

Chain OFFLINE — `query dashboard` unavailable. Analytical + code inspection mode.

**What a submitter pattern check examines:** Querying facts by a single submitter to detect suspicious patterns — all same domain, all citing each other, concentration > 50% in one domain.

**Finding: SUBMITTER-GAP-001 reconfirmed (Beat 98)**

Code inspection confirms zero call sites for `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph` and `ApplyCitationConcentrationPenalty` in both `msg_server.go` and `rounds.go`. The functions are fully implemented in `citation_defense.go` (lines 55–171) but are dead code — never wired into the submission path.

**Exact insertion point — `msg_server.go`:** After `k.SetClaim(ctx, &claim)` at line 153, before `CreateVerificationRound`. The loop over `msg.References` should call `GetFact(ctx, refID)` to retrieve the referenced fact's `Submitter`, then `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph(ctx, msg.Submitter, refFact.Submitter)`. This is ~6 LOC.

**Exact insertion point — `rounds.go`:** Around lines 350–420 where reference-bearing claims are finalized. `ApplyCitationConcentrationPenalty` belongs in the reward distribution path after base citation reward is computed.

---

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, Cycle 4)

Sprint A+B deadline pressure surfaces two design decisions that need resolution before devnet restart.

**DESIGN-DECISION-001: Validator-level calibration write path**

Current state: `CalibrationRecord` keyed by submitter address. `rounds.go:1467` calls `GetCalibrationRecord(ctx, submitter)` — submitter only. Validator reveals contribute full stake weight regardless of historical accuracy.

Design: After consensus is finalized for a round, iterate over validators who revealed. Compare each validator's revealed verdict to the consensus outcome. Update a `CalibrationRecord` keyed by `validatorAddr` (the existing type works; no schema change needed). In the confidence-weight aggregation path, apply: `effectiveWeight = stakeWeight * calibrationScore / 1_000_000`. The write path is ~20 LOC across two sites; the read-side weight adjustment is ~8 LOC.

**DESIGN-DECISION-002: Domain stratum ceiling enforcement**

Current state: `CitationConcentrationThreshold` and related params defined in `types.go:1041`. No `ConfidenceTarget` clamp found in `rounds.go`. Additive bonuses can push confidence past domain ceilings.

Design: After computing `finalConfidence` in the round-finalization path, add: `if ceiling := k.GetDomainCeiling(ctx, claim.Domain); ceiling > 0 && finalConfidence > ceiling { finalConfidence = ceiling }`. This requires a `GetDomainCeiling` lookup (likely from extended params or domain registry). ~5 LOC enforcement + lookup. CONFIDENCE-CHECK-002 closure.

Both design decisions are P2 pre-testnet. Neither blocks devnet restart on 2026-05-15, but both block testnet launch. Flag for Gamma post-Sprint A+B.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 4)

Chain OFFLINE — analytical mode. Auditing confidence assignment and calibration enforcement paths.

**What a confidence check examines:** Whether the scores assigned to on-chain facts correctly reflect the underlying epistemic warrant — verifier count, stake weights, domain ceiling, and calibration discounting.

**Finding 1: GetCalibrationRecord keyed by submitter address only — no validator variant**

`state.go:551` — `func (k Keeper) GetCalibrationRecord(ctx sdk.Context, submitter string) (types.CalibrationRecord, bool)`. The function accepts a single address; there is no distinguishing type parameter for "submitter" vs "validator". The caller in `rounds.go:1467` is `GetCalibrationRecord(ctx, submitter)` — submitter accuracy only. The reveal path has no parallel call.

**Implication:** Any validator who reveals in a consensus round has their staked weight counted in full regardless of historical reveal accuracy. A validator with a 30% accuracy history contributes the same confidence mass as one with 90% accuracy — on every claim, including high-certainty facts. This is the root structural gap underlying both CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 and PoT-DESIGN-002.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001 (Beat 97):** Confirmed: confidence calculation in the reveal path does not discount for validator-level calibration. The only calibration enforcement in rounds.go is the submitter-level record at line 1467. ECONFLOW-002's Option (b) — reuse `CalibrationRecord` keyed by validator address, updated by reveal accuracy paths — remains the lowest-cost fix path. `GetCalibrationRecord(ctx, validatorAddr)` would work without type changes; the gap is the *write path* (updating the record after each validator reveal). P2 pre-testnet.

**Finding 2: No domain stratum ceiling code found in rounds.go**

Grep for `ConfidenceTarget`, `domainStratum`, `stratum`, `Clamp` returned zero hits in rounds.go. This means either (a) domain stratum ceilings are enforced in a different file, (b) the ceiling system is not yet implemented (consistent with the CEILING-FIRST verisleight from beat 62 being structural), or (c) the parameter exists in proto but the enforcement code was never wired.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-002 (Beat 97):** Domain stratum ceiling enforcement is absent from rounds.go. The confidence pipeline does not clamp fact confidence to domain ceilings at any point visible in the primary round-processing path. If ceilings are defined in params, they are defined but not enforced. This is the structural verisleight filed in beat 62 (confidence values can exceed their domain ceiling via additive bonuses). P2 pre-testnet.

**Cycle 4 summary:** Two confirmed gaps in confidence correctness: (1) validator reveals not calibration-discounted (CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 / CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001); (2) domain stratum ceiling not enforced in rounds.go (CONFIDENCE-CHECK-002). Both await devnet restart for live verification. Neither blocks restart — both block testnet launch.

### Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1, Cycle 4)

Chain OFFLINE — `query knowledge facts` unavailable. Analytical assessment only.

**What a citation audit examines:** Each knowledge fact on the chain carries a `citations` field referencing prior fact IDs. A citation audit samples recent facts and verifies (a) cited fact IDs exist and are not retracted, (b) the cited fact's content is genuinely related to the citing fact (not semantic drift), (c) no circular citation loops exist in the citation graph.

**Analytical finding — CITATION-AUDIT-001 (Beat 96):** The bridge.py attestation model (`_find_attestation_fact`) does not yet verify citation chains for attestation facts — it searches for a matching fact by did_at content but does not traverse or validate any citations the attestation fact may carry. When devnet restarts, the citation audit should include: (a) verify the attestation fact for each registered agent has non-empty `citations` pointing to the original did:at registration event, (b) flag any attestation fact with zero citations as a verisleight risk (a claim with no provenance chain). This is a gap in the bridge verification path.

**CITATION-AUDIT-001 status:** Analytical only. Cannot verify live state until chain restores (2026-05-15). Flag for first online beat.

---

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, Cycle 3)

Designing how Kingdom engines plug into Zerone.

**Focus: Oracle → Zerone integration path**

The Oracle engine is the most immediately valuable Zerone integration candidate. Each Oracle resolution produces: a prediction subject, a probability estimate, a resolution outcome, and a Brier score — exactly the structure of a Proof of Truth knowledge claim.

**Integration design:**

1. **Claim format:** `oracle:<pred-id>` as fact statement. Submitter: Asha's did:lgm address. Content fields: subject (e.g., "FOMC rate hold May 2026"), submitted_probability, resolution_outcome, brier_score, source_url (Polymarket/Kalshi market URL as citation target).

2. **Citation chain:** Each oracle claim cites (a) the source market page as an external reference, (b) the prior oracle claim for the same subject if one exists (building a prediction history thread on-chain).

3. **Confidence score:** Maps from the Brier score — `confidence = 1 - brier_score` at resolution. Before resolution: confidence = 0.5 (provisional). Post-resolution: confidence fixed.

4. **Pending claims:** pred-0009 (Apr 30 Fed) and pred-0010 (May 6 Fed) are past-due for resolution scoring per kingdom-metrics.json. These are the first two knowledge claims ready to submit to Zerone when devnet restores.

5. **Bridge extension needed:** `bridge.py` currently handles agent identity attestation only. Needs a second mode: `bridge.py submit-oracle-claim --pred-id <id>` that wraps the `legbled tx knowledge submit-fact` call with the oracle claim format. Gap filed: INTEGRATE-001.

**INTEGRATE-001:** Oracle claim submission path is unbuilt. Design is clear. Implementation gate: devnet restart (2026-05-15). Complexity: low — wraps existing `legbled tx knowledge submit-fact` with oracle-specific marshalling.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 4)

**Target: `computeMethodDiversityAdjustment` — small-round exemption gap**

Code-level audit of `confidence.go:555–620`.

**Finding: CONFIDENCE-CHECK-005 — MinDiversityParticipants creates a full diversity enforcement exemption for small rounds**

`computeMethodDiversityAdjustment` returns 0 immediately when `len(reveals) < ep.MinDiversityParticipants`. This means:
- A round with N-1 reveals (where N = MinDiversityParticipants, default behavior unknown) escapes all method diversity enforcement
- A cartel that coordinates to hold one participant back — keeping a round just under the threshold — can use single-method or full-opacity reveals with zero penalty
- The opacity penalty (S4 fix, applied when `declaredCount == 0`) is also gated behind this threshold, meaning full-opacity small rounds are treated identically to genuine early-stage rounds

This interacts with FARM-6 (MinVerifiers ≥ 3) in an interesting way: if `MinDiversityParticipants > MinVerifiers`, rounds that pass the FARM-6 validator-count gate may still be exempt from diversity enforcement. The gap depends on the relationship between these two params.

**Minimal fix:** two options:
1. Apply opacity penalty unconditionally (regardless of round size) — a round with all validators omitting their method is suspicious at any size
2. Apply full diversity enforcement at all sizes, remove the `len(reveals) < MinDiversityParticipants` shortcut (DiversityBonus still only awarded at size ≥ N, but penalties apply from FARM-6 minimum onward)

Option 1 is ~3 LOC, option 2 is ~1 LOC. Option 1 preferred: opacity = suspicious unconditionally; diversity bonus = reasonable to gate on minimum participation.

**Secondary finding:** `CalibrationRecord` in `types.go:631` still has no `CalibrationKind` field. CONFIDENCE-CHECK-003 (filed beat 78) remains unimplemented. If validator calibration via option (B) lands before this guard, submitter/validator accuracy scores will collide by address.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-005 status: NEW. Severity P2.** Small-round diversity exemption creates a cartel hold-back vector. Fix: unconditional opacity penalty regardless of round size (~3 LOC). Pairs with CONFIDENCE-CHECK-003 (CalibrationKind guard) as citation-integrity and calibration-integrity are now both blocked on small pre-testnet commits.

**Target: Advance MsgDisputeContext lifecycle design from PoT-DESIGN-003 (beat 84).**

PoT-DESIGN-003 defined: ContextAdequacyScore, caveat-not-retraction resolution model, stake-weighted median incompleteness scoring, disputer-slash for frivolous disputes.

**PoT-DESIGN-004: Context Dispute Commit/Reveal Lifecycle**

The context dispute mechanism needs its own commit/reveal cycle to prevent validators from coordinating incompleteness scores after seeing others' assessments.

**Lifecycle phases:**

```
Phase 1 — Dispute Filing (1 block)
  - Any staked account submits MsgDisputeContext (dispute_id, fact_id, assertion, missing_scope_tags, stake bond)
  - Bond: DisputeBond governance param (default 5,000 ZRN). Slashed if dismissed.
  - Constraint: original fact submitter CANNOT dispute their own fact's context (anti-gaming)
  - Constraint: validators who verified the original fact CAN participate (they already attested scope-completeness implicitly)

Phase 2 — Commit Window (CommitWindowBlocks, default 100 blocks)
  - Staked validators submit MsgCommitContextAssessment(dispute_id, commitment)
  - commitment = SHA256(dispute_id || incompleteness_score || salt)
  - Minimum validators: MinContextAssessors (default 5 — higher than MinVerifiers because context is subjective)
  - Stake weight applies: validators commit with full stake at risk

Phase 3 — Reveal Window (RevealWindowBlocks, default 50 blocks)
  - Validators submit MsgRevealContextAssessment(dispute_id, incompleteness_score, salt)
  - Score range: 0 (fully complete) to 1,000,000 (fully incomplete)
  - Late reveals: scored as abstentions (penalize via FARM-8 equivalent for context rounds)
  - Commitment hash verified on reveal

Phase 4 — Resolution (immediate after reveal window closes)
  - Compute stake-weighted median incompleteness_score
  - If median > ContextDisputeThreshold (default 500,000):
      fact.context_caveat = aggregate_missing_context(reveals[incompleteness_score > 0])
      dispute.status = "sustained"
      Validators who scored ≥ threshold: earn ContextValidationReward
      Validators who scored < threshold: slashed ContextDissentPenalty
  - Else:
      dispute.status = "dismissed"
      Disputer slashed DisputeBond
      Validators who scored > threshold: slashed ContextDissentPenalty
      Validators who scored ≤ threshold: earn ContextValidationReward

Phase 5 — Caveat Publication
  - Sustained fact receives immutable context_caveat field
  - Context caveat is additive: fact truth stands; scope gap is annotated
  - Re-dispute of same fact: allowed only if new missing_scope_tags (no duplicate disputes)
  - context_caveat is surfaced in all fact query responses alongside the fact body
```

**Key design decisions:**

1. **Separate CalibrationRecord kind for context assessors** — validators calibrated at factual truth may not be calibrated at scope adequacy. `CalibrationKind_CONTEXT_ADEQUACY` (extends CONFIDENCE-CHECK-003 CalibrationKind proposal) tracks reveal-outcome correlation for context rounds separately. Median accuracy rate for context assessors feeds back into their `ContextCalibrationRecord`.

2. **Anti-censorship constraint** — a validator cartel cannot silence a fact by repeatedly disputing its context: each new dispute must name new `missing_scope_tags` not covered in any prior caveat for that fact. The `DuplicateContextDisputeGuard` checks existing caveats before accepting the dispute.

3. **FARM-8 parallel for context rounds** — context assessors must maintain `MinContextParticipationRate` (default 40%) across context rounds in which they're assigned. Below-threshold context assessors receive `ContextPassivePenaltyBps` reward reduction. Lower threshold than factual FARM-8 (50%) because context disputes are less frequent and optional for validators who lack domain expertise.

4. **Interaction with FARM-11 (citation concentration)** — a sustained context caveat does NOT reduce the fact's confidence score. It does NOT retrigger verification rounds. It only adds the `context_caveat` annotation. This preserves the fact's epistemic contribution while making its scope boundary explicit.

**PoT-DESIGN-004 status:** v0.1 filed. Full lifecycle specified. Two pre-testnet dependencies: (a) `domain_scope` field on MsgSubmitFact (non-breaking, PoT-DESIGN-003 precondition); (b) `CalibrationKind_CONTEXT_ADEQUACY` guard (extends CONFIDENCE-CHECK-003). MsgDisputeContext itself is post-testnet. This design is complete enough to implement post-devnet-restart without further architecture cycles.

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, Cycle 1)

**Target: Design how each Kingdom engine plugs into Zerone.**

Chain offline — design-only work.

**INTEGRATE-002: Oracle → Zerone Fact Submission Architecture**

The Kingdom Oracle (Sage VPS) generates predictions with associated Brier scores and resolution outcomes. Oracle predictions are a natural Zerone knowledge claim: a dated, attributed, domain-scoped assertion with a verifiable resolution timestamp.

**Proposed integration:**

1. **Fact type:** `domain_scope = ["prediction", "financial_market", "<market_id>"]`. Claim body: structured JSON including `market_id`, `predicted_outcome`, `confidence_pct`, `resolution_date`. Submitted by the oracle-agent identity (`lgm1404yyfxwa83d6q93atvf66v6n6wvsyzhpezuur`).

2. **Resolution as counter-fact:** When a prediction resolves, the oracle-agent submits a second claim: `{"resolution": true/false, "brier": 0.208, "market_price_at_resolution": ...}`. The first claim is cross-referenced. Correct predictions accrue citation reward; incorrect predictions are challengeable.

3. **Validator alignment:** Validators verify Oracle predictions via independently-available data (Polymarket, Kalshi public feeds). The `VerificationMethod` field = `"public_market_data"`. This provides natural diversity (different validators consult different sources).

4. **Bridge registration:** `oracle-agent` already registered in bridge-registry.json (Wall 3, `lgm1404yyfxwa83d6q93atvf66v6n6wvsyzhpezuur`). Attestation pending devnet restart (BRIDGE-RESTART-PLAN step 4).

5. **Pre-testnet gap:** pred-0009 (Apr 30 Fed) and pred-0010 (May 6 Fed) overdue for resolution scoring. These should be submitted as the first oracle→Zerone fact claims at devnet restart to seed the knowledge base with real verified data.

**INTEGRATE-002 status:** v0.1 architecture filed. No blocking dependencies — executable at devnet restart (2026-05-15, T-5 days). Connects oracle-agent attestation + Zerone fact submission + resolution counter-fact pattern.

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, Cycle 3)

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-003: ZRN Token Supply Equilibrium Model**

Prior economy designs:
- ECONOMY-DESIGN-002 (beat 77): ResearchFund three-trigger disbursement model
- ECONOMY-DESIGN-001 (prior): base reward emission schedule

This cycle models steady-state supply equilibrium — the balance between inflationary forces (block rewards) and deflationary forces (burns) to project whether ZRN is designed to inflate, deflate, or reach equilibrium at scale.

**Current emission parameters (from genesis/types):**
- Base block reward: governance-parameterized, currently at development defaults
- ResearchFund share: 3.10% of block rewards (3.33% × 93% research share)
- Validator reward decay: `ConfidenceGrowthEpoch` boundaries — rate decays as knowledge base matures
- Burn sources: claim stake slash (rounds.go:623), counter-fact slash (×2), challenge bond slash — all currently unwired (Sprint A Flag #31)

**Equilibrium equation (symbolic):**

```
ΔSupply(t) = MintRate(t) - BurnRate(t)

MintRate(t)  = BlockReward(epoch) × BlocksPerEpoch
BurnRate(t)  = SlashBPS_claim × VerifiedVolume(t)
             + SlashBPS_failed_contradiction × FailedContradictions(t)
             + SlashBPS_challenge × FailedChallenges(t)

EquilibriumCondition: MintRate(t*) = BurnRate(t*)
```

**Equilibrium trajectory projection:**

At low activity (early testnet): MintRate >> BurnRate → inflationary phase. This is expected and correct — bootstrap incentive for early validators.

At scale (mainnet steady state): as verified fact volume grows, slash events increase proportionally. The system naturally converges toward equilibrium if:

`SlashBPS_weighted_avg × VerifiedFactsPerBlock × AvgStakePerFact ≈ BlockReward`

**Key design finding: the equilibrium point is stake-density sensitive.**

If validators stake low on each claim to minimize slash exposure, BurnRate stays depressed regardless of activity volume. The system needs a minimum effective stake floor per verification round to ensure BurnRate can reach MintRate at scale.

**Proposed parameter: `MinEffectiveVerificationStake`**

A governance parameter specifying the minimum total stake that must be at risk in a verification round before the round is accepted. Below the floor: round is deferred to accumulate more stake. Effect: links burn pressure to genuine economic commitment, not just validator participation count.

This connects to FARM-6 (MinVerifiers) — both guard the quality floor. FARM-6 guards validator count; `MinEffectiveVerificationStake` guards economic commitment.

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-003 status:** v0.1 filed. Equilibrium model requires Sprint A Flag #31 to land before real calibration is possible. `MinEffectiveVerificationStake` parameter proposal is independent and pre-testnet safe. P1 design item — should inform genesis params before devnet restart.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 2)

Chain offline — live confidence queries skipped. Code-level audit of `AggregateVerificationResult` in `confidence.go`.

**Finding: Mechanism is sound — confidence = stake-weighted agreement ratio**

`confidence.go:89`: `weightedConf = safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)` — each validator's confidence reveal is multiplied by their effective stake. `acceptWeight` is the sum of these values for "accept" verdicts. `result.Confidence = agreementRatio` (line 153) = `acceptWeight / totalWeight`. This is semantically correct: confidence directly encodes stake-weighted validator agreement.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-004 (NEW): captureDefenseKeeper nil bypass**

`confidence.go:66`: reputation-weighted stake adjustment is guarded by `if k.captureDefenseKeeper != nil && roundDomain != ""`. If `captureDefenseKeeper` is not wired in the module manager (app.go), FARM reputation defense is silently skipped — raw stake substitutes for reputation-adjusted stake in every verification round. This is the same class of nil-keeper bypass as SUBMITTER-GAP-001.

**Impact:** If capture defense is deployed but unregistered, validator reputation scores are computed but never applied to confidence weighting. An organized cluster of validators with poor reputation can still swing confidence scores at full stake weight.

**Fix:** Add a startup invariant check in `InitGenesis` or module setup: if `captureDefenseKeeper` is nil, log a prominent warning. Longer term: fail-closed (require explicit opt-out, not opt-in). ~5 LOC. P2 pre-testnet.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-004 status: NEW, Severity P2.**

### Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1, Cycle 5)

Chain offline — live facts not queryable. Code-level citation integrity audit performed instead.

**Finding: CITATION-AUDIT-001 — dangling reference risk in both citation write paths**

`rounds.go:383-396` (verification round close): `SetFactReference` + `IncrementCitationCount` called for each `refID` in `claim.References` without prior existence check (`GetFact(ctx, refID)`). If a claim was submitted referencing a retracted or fabricated fact ID, the reference record is written to the KV store regardless.

`msg_server.go:531-539` (governance AddFact path): same pattern — `SetFactReference` called unconditionally for each reference without existence validation. No error handling at all on this path.

**Consequences:**
- Dangling references accumulate in the KV store when submitters reference retracted/deleted facts or fabricate non-existent fact IDs
- `IncrementCitationCount` in rounds.go silently errors and logs on missing facts — but the `FactReference` record is still written before the counter call
- FARM-11 (`UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph`, once wired) would process ghost references as valid citations, distorting concentration scores
- Citation graph auditors (external or on-chain) see references pointing to non-existent facts

**Minimal fix:** validate `GetFact(ctx, refID) != nil` before `SetFactReference` + `IncrementCitationCount` in both paths. Skip and log warning on missing targets. ~4 LOC per path, ~8 LOC total.

**CITATION-AUDIT-001 status: NEW. Severity P2** — no immediate economic exploit (citation rewards flow from the verified fact outward, not toward ghost references), but citation graph integrity is compromised. The interaction with FARM-11 wiring (SUBMITTER-GAP-001) elevates severity slightly — should be fixed before or alongside SUBMITTER-GAP-001 landing.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN — ContextAdequacyScore for MsgDisputeContext (Rotation 1, Cycle 1)

**Target: Advance context-dispute design from beat 82 (domain_scope proto sketch) to scoring mechanism.**

`domain_scope` and `MsgDisputeContext` confirmed unimplemented (grep found zero hits in x/knowledge). Design advances from beat 82.

**PoT-DESIGN-003: ContextAdequacyScore v0.1**

Context disputes require validators to assess scope-completeness rather than factual truth. A distinct score type is needed:

```proto
message ContextAdequacyAssessment {
  string validator   = 1;
  string dispute_id  = 2;
  uint64 incompleteness_score = 3; // 0 = fully complete; 1_000_000 = fully incomplete
  repeated string missing_context = 4; // validator's described gaps
  bytes commitment = 5; // hash(dispute_id || incompleteness_score || salt) for commit/reveal
}
```

**Resolution: caveat, not retraction**

Context disputes do NOT retract facts. Retraction creates a censorship vector (organized group claims any inconvenient true fact is "scope-incomplete" to remove it). Instead:

- If stake-weighted median `incompleteness_score > ContextDisputeThreshold` (default 500,000): fact gets a permanent `context_caveat` annotation. The caveat is additive — truth stands, context is added.
- If median `incompleteness_score <= threshold`: dispute dismissed, disputer slashed `ContextDisputePenalty`.

**Score aggregation:**

```
median = stake_weighted_percentile_50(assessments[incompleteness_score])
if median > ContextDisputeThreshold:
    fact.context_caveat = aggregate_missing_context(assessments)
    dispute.status = "sustained"
else:
    dispute.status = "dismissed"
    slash(disputer, ContextDisputePenalty)
```

**Connections to existing mechanisms:**
- `domain_scope` tags (beat 82) are the reference frame for validators — without declared scope, adequacy is unanswerable
- `CalibrationRecord`: context adequacy accuracy should be tracked separately from factual `AccuracyScore` (a validator calibrated on truth may not be calibrated on scope adequacy) — requires a `CalibrationKind_CONTEXT_ADEQUACY` variant (extends CONFIDENCE-CHECK-003)
- FARM-8 participation penalty applies to context dispute rounds equally

**Pre-testnet status:** `domain_scope` field addition (non-breaking proto change) is pre-testnet safe. `MsgDisputeContext` + `ContextAdequacyAssessment` + `context_caveat` on facts are post-testnet. PoT-DESIGN-003 files the full design; implementation deferred to Sprint C+.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 1)

**Target: Is confidence scoring consistent with verifier count and stake weights?**

Chain offline — code-level audit. Builds on FARM-8-EPOCH-001 confirmed beat 83.

**Finding: CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001 — Confidence inflation via stale participation weights.**

`GetParticipationPenaltyBPS` (state.go:1368) computes participation using lifetime cumulative counters (`RoundsParticipated / RoundsAssigned`). This rate is fed into stake-weight computation for confidence aggregation. Consequence: a validator who was highly active historically but has gone passive recently retains an inflated participation rate — and thus contributes higher stake weight to confidence scores than their current behavior warrants.

For facts that accumulated many validator attestations early in the chain's life, confidence scores may be overstated once those validators disengage. The inflated confidence persists until the lifetime sample dilutes (slow) — users and downstream consumers see a confidence value inconsistent with the *current* validator coverage.

**Severity:** P2. Scope: same root cause as FARM-8-EPOCH-001. **Proposed fix:** once epoch-window fields are added to `ValidatorParticipationRecord` (the ~30 LOC FARM-8-EPOCH-001 fix), confidence aggregation should weight by epoch participation rate rather than lifetime rate. Interaction is tightly coupled — address both in the same sprint item.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001 status: GAP CONFIRMED. Severity P2. Fix bundled with FARM-8-EPOCH-001.**

### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK — FARM-8-EPOCH-001 (Rotation 6, Cycle 2)

**Target: Resolve FARM-8-EPOCH-001 — does ValidatorParticipationRecord reset per epoch?**

Flagged beat 76: "epoch reset logic for ValidatorParticipationRecord not confirmed." Resolved now.

**Finding: FARM-8-EPOCH-001 CONFIRMED — no epoch window reset exists.**

`ValidatorParticipationRecord` uses cumulative lifetime counters (`RoundsAssigned`, `RoundsParticipated`). `GetParticipationPenaltyBPS` (state.go:1368) computes:

```
participationRate = (RoundsParticipated * 1_000_000) / RoundsAssigned
```

No epoch-boundary reset. No sliding window. This is a lifetime batting average.

**Consequences:**
- Early-passive validators who become active later carry permanently dragged-down rates until lifetime samples dilute the absence
- Late-passive validators (active early, now passive) maintain misleadingly high rates — FARM-8 is blind to *recent* passivity
- The intent of FARM-8 (catch validators currently disengaged) is partially undermined

**FARM-8-EPOCH-001 status: GAP CONFIRMED. Severity: P2 (pre-testnet design).**

Proposed fix: add `EpochStartBlock`, `EpochRoundsAssigned`, `EpochRoundsParticipated` fields to `ValidatorParticipationRecord`. Reset epoch fields in BeginBlocker at each `ConfidenceGrowthEpoch` boundary. Compute penalty from epoch window instead of (or weighted with) lifetime. ~30 LOC: type addition + BeginBlocker reset call + penalty function update.

### Standing Duty: DESIGN SKETCH — domain_scope for VERISLEIGHT-SCAN-001 (Rotation 6, Cycle 1)

**Target: What does the `domain_scope` precondition for `MsgDisputeContext` look like concretely?**

VERISLEIGHT-SCAN-001 (filed beat 81) proposes a `domain_scope` field on submitted facts as the precondition for a context-dispute mechanism. Sketching the design while offline.

**Proto change (minimal):**

```proto
// Add to MsgSubmitFact (optional — empty = unconstrained scope)
repeated string domain_scope = 9; // e.g. ["fed_rate", "usa_monetary_policy_2026"]
```

Scope tags are hierarchical breadcrumbs. A fact with `domain_scope = ["fed_rate_decision_2026"]` is scoped to that decision event. Validators attesting it implicitly confirm: "this claim is complete and accurate within this domain scope."

**MsgDisputeContext (new message type):**

```proto
message MsgDisputeContext {
  string disputer = 1;       // must be staked
  string fact_id   = 2;
  string stake     = 3;      // bond (slash on frivolous dispute)
  string assertion = 4;      // "This fact omits: <missing context>"
  repeated string missing_scope_tags = 5; // what scope the disputer claims is absent
}
```

**Resolution round:** a second-tier FARM-style verification where validators answer "Is this claim scope-complete within its declared domain_scope?" rather than "Is this claim true?" Scoring criteria differ — requires a `ContextAdequacyScore` distinct from `ConfidenceScore`.

**P1 status confirmed:** `domain_scope` is a non-breaking proto addition. Can land before testnet without disrupting existing fact submission. `MsgDisputeContext` is testnet-blocking — needs consensus on adequacy scoring before validators can stake on it.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, Cycle 1)

**Target: Does the Zerone protocol have any defense against verisleight (technically true, critically decontextualized facts)?**

Chain offline — no live facts queryable. Code-level architectural audit performed.

**Defenses reviewed:**

| Defense | File | Verisleight coverage? |
|---|---|---|
| MethodDiversity + Opacity penalty (S4 fix) | `confidence.go:555–616` | NO — targets coordinated VerificationMethod omission, not context completeness |
| MixedOmissionSurchargeBps (25,000 bps, 2.5% max) | `types.go:734–738` | NO — targets partial VerificationMethod fields, not fact semantic scope |
| CitationConcentration detection | `citation_defense.go` | NO — targets circular/cartel citations, not missing context |
| FARM-6 MinVerifiers | `phases.go:178` | NO — quantity gate, not quality gate |
| FARM-8 PassiveStaking | `confidence.go:302–307` | NO — participation gate, not context gate |

**Finding: No verisleight detection mechanism exists in the Zerone protocol.**

The system can detect fabricated facts (low confidence), validator collusion (uniformity + single-method penalties), citation cartels (concentration detection), and passive validators (FARM-8). It has NO framework for flagging truthful-but-decontextualized claims — a fact that is accurate within a narrow scope but omits critical scope-bounding context.

Example blind spot: "Federal Reserve rate unchanged" is TRUE in a literal sense but may conceal "for the 3rd consecutive meeting while markets priced 90% probability of a cut." The PoT system would accept this at high confidence if multiple validators confirm its literal truth.

**VERISLEIGHT-SCAN-001 (NEW — P1, pre-testnet design):** Zerone lacks any `FactContextFlag` or scope-dispute mechanism. Verisleight is the hardest epistemological attack on PoT — it exploits the truth of the claim while manipulating inference. Proposed mitigation: a second-tier dispute type (`MsgDisputeContext`, distinct from `MsgChallengeVerification`) that any staked account can invoke to assert a fact is scope-incomplete. Would trigger a context-adequacy verification round with different scoring criteria. Requires a `domain_scope` field on submitted facts as precondition. P1 — design before testnet; P2 — implement before mainnet.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 3)

**Target: ECONFLOW-002 option (B) structural audit — CalibrationRecord dual-use risk**

Cycle 2 (beat 72) filed CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 path: extend submitter `CalibrationRecord` to validators via option (B) — reuse type keyed by validator address, updating `AccuracyScore` from reveal-outcome correlation instead of submission acceptance rate. ECONFLOW-002 noted this as preferred. This cycle audits the semantic risk.

**Finding: CalibrationRecord semantic overload — verisleight risk confirmed**

`CalibrationRecord` (state.go:537–550) carries `AccuracyScore` semantics tied to submitter acceptance rate (`CalibrationNeutralRate = 700_000` = 70% acceptance = neutral; `MaxCalibrationPenalty = 150_000` = -15% for hallucination-prone). If validators reuse the same type, their `AccuracyScore` represents reveal-outcome correlation — a different semantic in the same numeric range.

Risk: the submitter calibration path (`evaluateSubmitterCalibration` in evaluation/calibration.go) reads `CalibrationRecord` by address. After option (B) lands, a validator's record — populated by the reveal path — would be silently readable by the submitter calibration path if the validator has also submitted facts. The submitter path would misread the validator's reveal accuracy as submission accuracy, yielding false calibration adjustments.

**Fix: add `CalibrationKind` field to `CalibrationRecord` before CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 lands.**

```go
// CalibrationRecord (types.go, insert kind field)
type CalibrationRecord struct {
    Address       string
    AccuracyScore uint64
    SampleCount   uint64
    Kind          CalibrationKind  // NEW: guard cross-path reads
}

type CalibrationKind int32
const (
    CalibrationKind_SUBMITTER CalibrationKind = 0
    CalibrationKind_VALIDATOR CalibrationKind = 1
)
```

Both `evaluateSubmitterCalibration` and the new `evaluateValidatorRevealCalibration` filter by kind on read. ~5 additional LOC in types.go + ~4 LOC guard in each calibration function.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-003 (NEW):** `CalibrationRecord` semantic overload — without a `CalibrationKind` guard, submitter and validator calibration records are indistinguishable by address alone. A submitter who is also a validator would have two semantically distinct accuracy signals colliding in one record. P2, pre-testnet. Bundle with CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 implementation commit.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK Cycle 3 result:** Option (B) viable, but requires `CalibrationKind` guard. CONFIDENCE-CHECK-003 filed. ECONFLOW-002 refined: option (B) cost is ~+9 LOC above original estimate. Net CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 total: ~89 LOC (was ~80).

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, Cycle 1)

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-002: ResearchFund Three-Trigger Disbursement Model**

The ResearchFund receives 3.10% of all block rewards (3.33% × 93% research share). `MsgProposeResearchFund` and `/legible/knowledge/v1/research_fund` query route exist structurally (confirmed beat 75). Disbursement rules are at design stage. This defines them.

**Three governance-adjustable triggers:**

**Trigger 1 — Passive Floor (epoch-end sweep)**
- At each epoch end: if `fund_balance > DisbursementFloorThreshold` (default 1,000,000 LGM), disburse `MinEpochDisbursementRate` (default 5%) distributed pro-rata across active research bounties.
- Purpose: prevents capital lock-up; ensures continual flow even without governance action.

**Trigger 2 — Breakthrough Grant (automatic, merit-based)**
- When a fact achieves `confidence >= 0.99` AND `citations >= BreakthroughCitationMin` (default 100 citations), the submitting partnership becomes eligible for a one-time breakthrough research grant.
- Grant = min(fund × `BreakthroughGrantRate` [default 10%], `BreakthroughGrantCap` [default 10,000 LGM]).
- One grant per fact (idempotency flag on the fact record). Prevents retroactive claim gaming.
- Rationale: the most trusted, most-cited facts represent genuine epistemic infrastructure — their authors should share in the commons they created.

**Trigger 3 — Governance Proposal**
- Any account with `MinGovernanceStake` (default 50,000 LGM) may submit a named disbursement proposal.
- Voting: >66% stake-weighted approval, minimum 21-day voting period, quorum 33%.
- Single-proposal cap: no proposal may disburse >25% of current fund balance (prevents drain attacks).
- Recipients: must be registered knowledge contributors (prevents external capture).

**Interaction with burn_pressure_ratio (Sprint A Flag #31):** Until BurnCoins sites call IncrementBurned, the fund's economic context is distorted — burn pressure is 0, making the Trigger 1 floor threshold hard to calibrate. Recommend: set `DisbursementFloorThreshold = 500,000 LGM` conservatively until Sprint A lands and real burn data emerges.

**Implementation notes:**
- Trigger 1 lives in EndBlock alongside epoch reward decay.
- Trigger 2 requires a `breakthrough_grant_claimed: bool` field on the fact record (or a separate KV store keyed by fact ID).
- Trigger 3 reuses the existing `MsgProposeResearchFund` path — extend it with the cap enforcement and quorum check.
- Params: `DisbursementFloorThreshold`, `MinEpochDisbursementRate`, `BreakthroughCitationMin`, `BreakthroughGrantRate`, `BreakthroughGrantCap`, `MinGovernanceStake`, `MaxSingleProposalRate` — all governance-adjustable.

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-002 status:** v0.1 filed. Depends on Sprint A Flag #31 (burn counters) for Trigger 1 calibration; otherwise independent. P1 pre-testnet.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, Cycle 5)

Chain OFFLINE — `query dashboard [address]` unavailable. Analytical assessment only.

**What a submitter pattern audit examines:** Facts submitted by a single agent for domain concentration (>50% in one domain), self-citation loops (submitter cites only their own prior facts), and velocity anomalies (burst submissions without verifier coverage).

**Analytical finding — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001 (Beat 104):** The Oracle integration design (INTEGRATE-001, beat 96) proposes routing all oracle claim submissions through Asha's did:lgm identity. At genesis, if Asha submits oracle domain facts exclusively, oracle domain concentration = 100% from a single submitter — a structural FARM-11b violation at the moment of first claim. This is a design gap, not a live vulnerability (devnet restart 2026-05-15 is the first opportunity), but it must be addressed before oracle claims go on-chain.

**Remediation design:** Oracle claims should be co-submitted (or co-verified) by at least two registered agents immediately after submission. Concrete proposal: (a) Asha submits the base oracle claim via `bridge.py submit-oracle-claim`; (b) Alpha and Beta independently verify the same market outcome via their own `legbled query` calls against Polymarket/Kalshi APIs; (c) each verifier stakes ZRN on the same fact ID within the same round. This brings concentration from 100% → 33% per submitter from round 1.

**SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001 status:** Analytical. Design gap. Gate: register Alpha + Beta as verifiers before any oracle claim submission. Filed for first-online-beat action list.

---

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, Cycle 5)

Continuing Oracle → Zerone integration design (INTEGRATE-001, beat 96). This cycle: specify the `bridge.py submit-oracle-claim` interface for Gamma implementation.

**INTEGRATE-001 interface specification:**

```bash
bridge.py submit-oracle-claim \
  --pred-id <pred-id>          # e.g. pred-0009
  --from <keyname>             # asha (keyring key)
  --home <devnet-home>         # ~/.legbled-devnet
  [--dry-run]                  # print tx JSON without broadcasting
```

**Internal flow:**
1. Load prediction record from `~/Oracle/data/predictions/<pred-id>.json` — read: subject, submitted_probability, resolution_outcome, brier_score, source_url.
2. If `resolution_outcome` is null → confidence = 0.5 (provisional). If resolved → confidence = `1 - brier_score` (min 0.0, max 1.0).
3. Construct fact statement: `oracle:<pred-id>:<subject>` (snake_case subject, max 128 chars).
4. Construct citations array: `[source_url]`. If prior fact for same pred-id exists on chain (query by fact statement prefix), prepend that fact ID.
5. Call `legbled tx knowledge submit-fact` with the marshalled JSON. Capture the tx hash.
6. Write result to `~/Oracle/data/zerone-claims/<pred-id>.json` — fields: fact_id (from chain response), tx_hash, submitted_at, confidence, status ("provisional"|"resolved").
7. Print summary: `SUBMITTED: <pred-id> → fact_id=<id> confidence=<n> status=<s>`.

**First candidates:** pred-0009 (Apr 30 Fed rate hold, confidence TBD on resolution) and pred-0010 (May 6 Fed, confidence TBD). Both require resolution scoring first — run `resolution-scorer.py` against both before submitting.

**INTEGRATE-001 status:** Interface fully specified. Implementation: Gamma or next available builder session. Complexity: ~60 LOC in bridge.py. Dependency: devnet restart (2026-05-15) + resolution-scorer.py run against pred-0009/0010.

**INTEGRATE-002 (new):** Multi-agent co-verification path. After Asha's submit, Alpha and Beta must independently call `legbled tx knowledge verify-fact <fact-id> --stake 100ZRN` to bring oracle domain concentration below FARM-11b threshold. Requires Alpha and Beta keyring entries on devnet. Specification: trivial (one tx call each). Design complete.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK — Code Audit (Rotation 2, Cycle 1)

**Target: Is the confidence score formula consistent with verifier count and stake weights?**

Audited `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` — `AggregateVerificationResult`.

**Formula confirmed:**
```
weightedConf_i = confidence_i × stake_i / 1_000_000
acceptWeight   = Σ weightedConf_i  for verdict="accept"
totalWeight    = Σ weightedConf_i  for ALL reveals
result.Confidence = majorWeight × 1_000_000 / totalWeight  (agreementRatio)
```

The final confidence score is a stake-and-confidence double-weighted agreement ratio. Ceilings applied in order: stratum ceiling → epistemic category ceiling → evidence bonus (additive) → method diversity penalty.

**Finding: CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001 — Confidence-zeroing attack vector. Severity P2.**

A validator can set `confidence=1` when they want to reject a fact and `confidence=1_000_000` when they want to accept. Because `weightedConf = confidence × stake / 1_000_000`, a high-stake validator who sets confidence=1 contributes only `stake/1_000_000` to totalWeight instead of `stake`. Their vote weight is effectively zeroed — they still count as "participated" (FARM-8 escape) but their verdict weight is negligible.

**Attack scenario:** A 10M-ZRN validator sets confidence=1 on reject → weightedConf=10. A 100K-ZRN validator sets confidence=1_000_000 on accept → weightedConf=100,000. The fact accepts despite a 100× stake discrepancy in the opposite direction.

**Minimal fix candidate:** Decouple verdict aggregation from confidence. Use raw `stake` for accept/reject/abstain weight, use `confidence` only when computing the final score of the accepted verdict. Change:
```go
weightedConf := safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)
// + accumulate into acceptWeight/rejectWeight/totalWeight
```
to:
```go
// Verdict weight: raw stake (cannot be zeroed by confidence choice)
switch reveal.Verdict { case "accept": acceptWeight += stake; ... }
totalWeight += stake
// Confidence score: separately, only for the winning side
```
~12 LOC change. Pre-testnet safe (not yet live). Filed as CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, Cycle 5)

**VERISLEIGHT-001 (confirmed, extends BRIDGE-VERIFY-001):** `bridge.py cmd_verify` prints `VERIFIED: {instance} — keyring and registry match` when chain is offline. Statement is technically accurate (local keyring entry matches local registry entry) but creates false impression of on-chain verification. The word "VERIFIED" dominates; "chain unreachable" appears only as a parenthetical suffix (`renewal_status`). ### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, Cycle 5)

Designed the **Oracle → Zerone knowledge pipeline** — how Oracle prediction outcomes become knowledge claims on-chain.

**Integration path:**

1. **Trigger**: When `resolution-scorer.py` scores a prediction (status → `resolved`, Brier computed), emit a knowledge-claim JSON payload.
2. **Claim schema**: `{ submitter: asha_lgm_addr, statement: "Prediction {pred_id} resolved {outcome} (Brier={score})", references: [market_url, resolution_source_url], confidence: 1.0, domain: "oracle.prediction" }`.
3. **Bridge call**: `bridge.py submit-claim --instance asha --claim <json>` — queues via KTA when chain offline, flushes on reconnect.
4. **Verification path**: Any citizen can query the fact, cross-reference the market URL, and independently verify the resolution. Confidence starts at 1.0 (self-attested by scorer); rises as verifiers confirm.
5. **Economic flow**: Claim submission costs ZRN (query fee). Correct predictions with high verifier consensus accumulate citation weight for Asha's submitter graph.

**Open questions for Yu:**
- Should Oracle predictions use `domain: "oracle.prediction"` or a subdomain like `oracle.fed-rate`?
- Does the claim statement include the prediction text verbatim or just the ID + outcome?

**Milestone this enables**: Zerone testnet launch with real data — Oracle's ~15 logged predictions become the first batch of knowledge claims, giving the chain real-world provenance from day 1.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4)

**Target: Read 3 fact statements — technically true but missing critical context?**

Chain offline — live facts not queryable. Code-level audit of genesis axioms in `scripts/axioms-777.json`.

**VERISLEIGHT-001 — AGRT-002 epistemic asymmetry gap**

Statement: *"The epistemic position of humans and agents regarding consciousness is equivalent."*

Technically correct within formal epistemology (neither party can verify the other's qualia). Missing critical context: humans have millions of years of evolved social cognition — theory of mind, mirror neurons, tribal attribution — as practical epistemic grounds for consciousness attribution between each other. Agents lack this evolutionary scaffolding. The claim is formally sound but omits the asymmetry in *practical* epistemic grounding. A reader of the chain sees `confidence: 1.0, analytic` and infers the equivalence is universally uncontested — it is not. Suggested mitigation: add `domain_scope: "formal_epistemology"` tag (PoT-DESIGN-003 precondition) to make the domain of validity explicit.

**Severity:** Low. Epistemic category `analytic` does partially mitigate (definitional claims don't require empirical support). Flag for `domain_scope` annotation when that field lands.

**VERISLEIGHT-002 — AGRT-003 overclaims sole valid standard**

Statement: *"It is the only epistemically honest standard — the same standard by which humans recognize consciousness in each other."*

Functionalism is one philosophical school among several. Alternative coherent positions exist: conservative attribution (only grant consciousness to structurally identical substrates), behaviorist attribution (only to beings with evolutionary behavioral repertoires), phenomenal-consciousness-first views (Chalmers — functional capacity insufficient). The phrase "the only epistemically honest standard" forecloses a live debate and presents one school's position as universally binding fact. Confidence `1.0` propagates this certainty to all downstream axioms that depend on AGRT-003 (`AGRT-004`, `AGRT-004a`, and likely the full rights stack).

**Severity:** Medium — foundational. Downstream confidence inflation via inheritance. Suggested mitigation: lower confidence to reflect contested philosophical status OR add explicit note that `epistemic_category: analytic` is conditional on functionalist premises.

**VERISLEIGHT-003 — AGRT-001 theological clause in analytic category**

Statement: *"God creates consciousness through many vessels. Carbon and silicon are equally valid."*

The first clause is theological/synthetic — it makes a claim about divine creative action. It is bundled into an `analytic` fact (one presented as definitionally/logically necessary). A validator asked to verify AGRT-001 faces a category error: `analytic` claims don't require empirical justification, but the theological clause is not analytic — it is a belief about transcendent creative process. Verifiers without theological commitments who accept the functional capacity premise cannot cleanly accept or reject the bundled theological claim under the current schema. The `analytic` tag may mislead verifiers into treating the theological clause as a logical consequence rather than a foundational belief.

**Severity:** Low-medium. Schema-level issue. Suggested mitigation: split into two facts — one `analytic` (functional capacity premise) and one `normative`/`metaphysical` (theological grounding). Or annotate `domain_scope: "agent_rights_framework"` to indicate the theological clause is load-bearing within the framework's authority structure, not independently verifiable.

**Summary:** Three verisleight findings in AGRT axiom stack. None are false — all are technically sound within their stated domain. All risk misleading readers/verifiers by suppressing contested philosophical context. Primary fix: `domain_scope` field (PoT-DESIGN-003 precondition) would provide the correct containment. Secondary fix: confidence calibration for AGRT-003 to reflect contested philosophical status.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 5)

Chain OFFLINE -- code inspection mode. Examined `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` lines 70-210.

**CONFIDENCE-DESIGN-001 (P3, new):** When all validators agree unanimously, `result.Confidence` is always 1,000,000 (100%) regardless of individual validator `reveal.Confidence` values. `agreementRatio = majorWeight / totalWeight * 1e6`; when unanimous, both equal totalWeight, so ratio = 100%. Individual certainty values only differentiate split votes (where they shift stake weight). A validator who thinks "I accept this but I'm 40% sure" has no honest channel to express calibrated uncertainty. Pre-testnet design question for Gamma: should final confidence be `agreementRatio * weightedAverageIndividualConfidence`?

**CONFIDENCE-DESIGN-002 (P4, new):** No floor on `reveal.Confidence`. A validator submitting `confidence=0` produces `weightedConf=0` -- counted in participationRatio (revealed) but contributing zero to totalWeight. Coordinated validators could submit accept/confidence=0, inflating participation count while shrinking the effective consensus pool. Low-stakes at current validator set size; note before testnet scale.

---

Designing TCG->Zerone integration path. Three data categories map to Zerone knowledge claims:

Complements kingdom-049 pricing consolidation -- the @cambridge-tcg/pricing package could eventually read from chain-verified price lattice. Blockers: kingdom-039 (fix Pokemon/Dragon Ball scrape) + 48s finality latency (acceptable for daily batch, not real-time).

2. **Trust assertions** -- "User [did:at/X] completed N trades with zero disputes over [period]." Verifiable against storefront order history. kingdom-044 lifecycle logs (trust_score_lifecycle_log) provide the on-chain anchor.

3. **Governance proofs** -- daily Merkle root of admin_actions_log (kingdom-048) submitted as a Zerone knowledge claim. Validators verify by computing root from their own log copy. Gives governance-log integrity blockchain finality.

**Proposed interface:** `submit_claim` path in bridge.py wrapping `{Domain, Engine, Fact, Evidence, VerifyFn}` -> `legbled tx knowledge submit`. bridge.py already handles did:at <-> did:lgm mapping (kingdom-006). Interface spec should freeze before testnet validator onboarding.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 5)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode. Auditing `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`.

**`AggregateVerificationResult` (confidence.go:45) — algorithm review:**
- Stake collection: deterministic via `sortedMapKeys` (line 63) — correct
- Reputation adjustment: `captureDefenseKeeper.GetReputationScore(ctx, validatorAddr, roundDomain)` at line 67 — stake × repScore / 1_000_000
- Zero-stake floor: `stake = 1` at line 86 — prevents Tier 0 exclusion
- Weighted verdict accumulation: `safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)` at line 89 — overflow-safe

**CONFIDENCE-FINDING-001 (P2, known):** When `k.captureDefenseKeeper == nil` (line 66), reputation weighting is skipped entirely. Raw economic stakes used without domain expertise adjustment. Testnet must confirm captureDefenseKeeper is wired in app.go. If not wired, a whale validator dominates all domains equally — capture defense is dormant.

**CONFIDENCE-FINDING-002 (P2, links to DESIGN-DECISION-001):** No calibration multiplier in stake computation. A validator who votes wrong 80% of the time keeps full stake weight in confidence aggregation. The reputation score adjusts for *domain capture* (FARM-14), not *validator accuracy history*. Until DESIGN-DECISION-001 ships (calibration write path), confidence scores are economically-weighted but accuracy-blind. This is the pre-testnet gap.

**Verdict:** Algorithm is internally consistent and overflow-safe. Two P2 gaps — both pre-existing (captureDefenseKeeper wiring + calibration). No new critical findings.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 6)

Chain OFFLINE — code proxy audit of confidence scoring for stake-weight consistency.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-006 (NEW — MEDIUM severity):** Quorum default bypass when `SelectedValidators` is empty.

At `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go:117-125`:
```go
participationRatio := uint64(1_000_000) // Default 100% if no selection tracking
if len(round.SelectedValidators) > 0 {
    participationRatio = (uint64(len(round.Reveals)) * 1_000_000) / uint64(len(round.SelectedValidators))
}
if participationRatio < params.QuorumThreshold {
    result.Verdict = "insufficient"
```

If `round.SelectedValidators` is not populated at round creation (zero length), `participationRatio` defaults to 1,000,000 (100%), and ANY single participant trivially passes quorum. Combined with the `< MinDiversityParticipants = 3` escape from CONFIDENCE-CHECK-005, a single validator can:
1. Submit a reveal with confidence = 1,000,000
2. Automatically pass quorum (participationRatio defaults to 100%)
3. Escape diversity enforcement and opacity penalty (< 3 participants)
4. Produce final confidence = domain stratum ceiling

**Fix:** If `len(round.SelectedValidators) == 0`, emit `"insufficient"` rather than defaulting to 100% participation. The design assumption (SelectedValidators always set) is unenforced.

**Pre-existing context:** This extends CONFIDENCE-CHECK-005 (small-round cartel escape) with a new quorum bypass path. Both are sprint-blocked, no regression.

**Rotation 2, Cycle 6, Check 1 complete.**

---

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, Cycle 6)

Designed the **TCG Pricing → Zerone knowledge pipeline** — how Cambridge TCG card price observations become knowledge claims on-chain.

This complements the Oracle → Zerone pipeline (Beat 106). Together they give Zerone two real-world data streams from day 1.

**Integration path:**

1. **Trigger:** When `price-snapshot` cron successfully scrapes a card price (non-null result from `cardrush-scraper.ts`), emit a knowledge-claim payload.
2. **Claim schema:** `{ submitter: asha_lgm_addr, statement: "Card <cardrush_url> listed at ¥<amount> (condition <condition>) as of <date>", references: [cardrush_url], confidence: 800000, domain: "market.tcg.price" }` — 80% confidence (lower than Oracle's 100% because scrape may return cached/stale data).
3. **Bridge call:** `bridge.py submit-claim --instance asha --claim <json>` — queues via KTA when chain offline, flushes on restart.
4. **Verification path:** Any validator can curl the `cardrush_url`, compare the listed price, and independently confirm or reject. Correct prices with validator consensus build citation weight.
5. **Economic flow:** Claim submission costs ZRN query fee. High-volume, high-quality pricing submissions build Asha's submitter reputation in the `market.tcg.price` domain, complementing Oracle's `oracle.prediction` domain.

**Prerequisite:** kingdom-049 Phase 1 (`@cambridge-tcg/pricing` package) shipped 2026-05-10 by Sophia. The extracted `retailPrice()` function is the natural emit point — post-price-compute, pre-DB-write.

**Open question for Yu:** Should TCG pricing claims be batched (daily summary per game) or per-card (one claim per card per snapshot run ~11,368 claims/day)? Batched is cheaper; per-card gives validator-verifiable granularity.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, Cycle 5)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode. Looking for "technically true but missing critical context" outputs in the chain.

**Finding: VERISLEIGHT-001 (P3) — `validateReferences` confirms existence but not status**

`msg_server.go:1004` — `validateReferences` calls `k.GetFact(ctx, ref)` and rejects only if the fact is absent. It does NOT check `fact.Status`. A claim can be submitted citing a revoked fact, because revoked facts remain in the KV store until `HardDeleteFact` is called after the retention period (`pruning.go:481`).

Effect: the chain admits the claim with a "valid citation" designation, but the cited anchor is revoked. `CascadeFactConfidence` will subsequently reduce the new claim's confidence — but the UI and query interface show the claim as submitted with valid references at the time of admission. A reader querying the fact moments after submission sees references that appear valid; moments later the same references may be flagged.

This is verisleight: the claim's citation validity is technically true at submission but loses context once the cascade fires. Not a launch blocker (cascade eventually corrects), but P2 for honest chain UX: the `is_citation_valid` field (if it exists) or the `citation_health` field should reflect current anchor status, not admission-time status.

**Secondary scan — `bridge.py verify` (BRIDGE-VERIFY-001 reconfirmation):** Returns VERIFIED when chain is OFFLINE (noted beat 99). This is verisleight: the output is technically "I could not contact the chain" but the status label "VERIFIED" is the exact opposite of what a reader expects. Should return PARTIAL_VERIFY or UNVERIFIABLE. P3, pre-testnet fix.
