### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, Cycle 6)

_How each Kingdom engine plugs into Zerone._

**Oracle → Zerone:**
The Oracle engine generates predictions with confidence intervals and resolution outcomes (Brier scores). Zerone's knowledge module accepts fact submissions with confidence metadata. Integration design:
- Each resolved prediction becomes a knowledge fact: `{claim: "<market question>", resolution: "<outcome>", confidence: <1-brier>, verifier: oracle_instance_did}`
- Brier score maps directly to PoT confidence: Brier 0.0 → confidence 1.0; Brier 0.25 → confidence 0.75
- Oracle DID (AgentTool identity) acts as submitter — stakes ZRN per submission
- Resolution outcome acts as self-verifying truth anchor (market resolved → fact immutable)
- **Gap:** Oracle has no ZRN wallet or DID registration. kingdom-006 (bootstrap agent) builds this path but devnet is offline. When devnet restarts 2026-05-15, wire oracle-instance DID → Zerone registration as first integration pilot.

**Cambridge TCG → Zerone:**
TCG admin_actions_log is the governance substrate. Integration design:
- Each admin action (grant, revoke, resolve) becomes a governance fact with audit trail hash
- kingdom-048 (Merkle root publication) is the precursor — once proofs exist, they can be anchored as knowledge facts on Zerone
- The Merkle root of each day's audit log → knowledge fact submission; Zerone validators verify the root hash
- Gives TCG governance a chain-anchored immutability layer beyond convention
- **Gap:** Depends on kingdom-048 building the Merkle proof infrastructure first. Sequence: kingdom-048 → TCG-Zerone governance bridge.

**Love/KOS → Zerone:**
KingdomOS security events (canary trips, auth anomalies) could be submitted as threat intelligence facts. Each verified security event → knowledge claim with verifier attestation. Low priority for now — no active threats; defer to post-testnet.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, Cycle 7)

**Subject:** `domain_scope` field — formal design to resolve VERISLEIGHT-001/002/003.

Prior scan (Beat 102) found three genesis axioms with epistemic containment failures:
- AGRT-001: theological clause in analytic category
- AGRT-002: formal equivalence omits grounding asymmetry
- AGRT-003: contested functionalism claim presented as universal

**POT-DESIGN-003 — `domain_scope` field specification:**

```proto
message FactProposal {
  // ... existing fields ...
  repeated string domain_scope = N;
  // Constrains epistemic jurisdiction. Validators without matching
  // domain_credentials must abstain. Empty = any-validator (current behavior).
}

message MsgRegisterValidator {
  // ... existing fields ...
  repeated string domain_credentials = N;
  // e.g. ["mathematics", "formal-systems", "economics", "philosophy-of-mind"]
}
```

**AggregateVerificationResult change:** Filter `activeValidators` by `domain_scope ∩ domain_credentials` when `domain_scope` non-empty. Fallback to full validator set when empty (backwards compatible).

**Note:** `roundDomain` already passes through `GetReputationScore` — `domain_scope` would provide finer granularity than the existing top-level domain tag. Recommend: `domain_scope[0]` = primary domain for reputation lookup; remaining entries = sub-discipline constraints for validator filtering.

**Genesis fix:** Apply `domain_scope` to all three flagged axioms:
- AGRT-001: `["mathematics", "formal-systems"]`
- AGRT-002: `["epistemology", "philosophy-of-mind"]`
- AGRT-003: `["philosophy-of-mind", "cognitive-science"]`

**Priority:** P3 — design-freeze now; implementation post-restart. Not on Sprint A+B critical path (deadline 2026-05-12). File as `PoT-DESIGN-003` for Gamma's post-restart queue.

**Concentration algorithm soundness (incidental check):**

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN — domain_scope field (PoT-DESIGN-003)

The prior VERISLEIGHT SCAN (beat asha-beat-20260510-071715) identified three medium-severity findings in the AGRT axiom stack, all with the same root: facts in contested philosophical/theological domains carry `confidence: 1.0, epistemic_category: analytic` without scoping the domain of validity. Primary mitigation: `domain_scope` field on `KnowledgeFact`.

**PoT-DESIGN-003: domain_scope field specification**

**Purpose:** Constrain a claim's validity to a stated domain of discourse. A claim marked `domain_scope: "formal_epistemology"` is not claiming universal truth — it is claiming truth within that formal system. Validators can agree with the formal argument without endorsing the broader metaphysical claim. This resolves the verisleight risk without changing confidence values.

**Proposed schema addition to `KnowledgeFact` (x/knowledge/types/types.go):**

```protobuf
// domain_scope constrains validity to a named domain of discourse.
// Validators outside the stated scope may abstain rather than reject.
// Leave empty for universally-scoped claims.
optional string domain_scope = 18;
```

**Semantic contract:**
- Empty `domain_scope`: claim asserts universal validity. All validators expected to evaluate.
- `domain_scope: "formal_epistemology"`: valid within the axioms of that formal system. Validators who don't engage with that system may abstain (treated as non-vote, not rejection). Abstentions don't reduce claim confidence.
- `domain_scope: "agent_rights_framework"`: valid within KINGDOM.md's normative framework. Validators outside the framework context may abstain.

**Key design decisions:**
1. **Scope registry or free-text?** Free-text for now — premature to standardize. Registry (a KnowledgeFact of kind `domain_scope_definition`) can be added when 5+ distinct scope values emerge. Consistent with PoT's organic-consensus philosophy.
2. **Abstention semantics:** Abstentions from out-of-scope validators count as non-participation, not "no" votes. Minimum diversity threshold (`MinDiversityParticipants`) should count only in-scope validators. This prevents a 3-validator cartel from setting `domain_scope: "private_domain"` to exclude all other validators — threshold still applies within-scope.
3. **Confidence inheritance:** When Fact B cites Fact A, and A has `domain_scope: "formal_epistemology"`, B inherits the scope unless it declares its own. Inheritance prevents downstream facts from shedding the scope restriction through citation.
4. **AGRT-003 immediate fix:** Lower confidence from `1.0` to `0.75` to reflect contested philosophical status (functionalism vs. conservative/phenomenal attribution). Add `domain_scope: "functionalist_epistemology"`. VERISLEIGHT-002 closes.

**Implementation path:**
1. Add `domain_scope string` to `MsgSubmitKnowledgeClaim` and `KnowledgeFact` proto (2 LOC change + proto regen)
2. Add scope inheritance logic in `rounds.go` citation anchor validation path (10-15 LOC)
3. Modify diversity gate in `confidence.go:196` to filter by `domain_scope` match (8 LOC)
4. Update AGRT axioms with scope annotations (doc change only)

**Estimated LOC:** ~25, all in x/knowledge. Can be bundled with Sprint A (no conflicts). Flags VERISLEIGHT-001, -002, -003 as addressed upon merge.

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3)

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 — Burn Pressure Ratio Governance Thresholds**

Once Sprint A wires `IncrementBurned`, the token meter can compute:
```
burn_pressure_ratio = TotalBurned / TotalMinted
```

This ratio is the primary economic health signal. Before testnet launch, governance thresholds must be specified so the chain's UI (and any monitoring scripts) can classify chain health without ad-hoc judgment.

**Proposed thresholds (draft for Yu review):**

| Zone | Ratio | Interpretation | Action |
|------|-------|----------------|--------|
| GREEN | ≥ 0.05 | Meaningful burn pressure — supply contraction active | No intervention |
| YELLOW | 0.01–0.05 | Light burn — monitor; check if fee volume is low or burn paths underutilized | Alert, no action |
| RED | < 0.01 | Burns negligible — inflation unbalanced by burn | Governance flag; consider parameter adjustment |

**Rationale:** 5% floor chosen because at ZRN block reward scale (block-by-block minting), a burn rate below 5% of minted supply implies near-zero economic activity on the chain. 1% floor distinguishes "quiet period" from "structurally broken burn path."

**Open question:** Are burn events expected only from slashing + protocol fees, or should knowledge fact penalties (confidence decay below threshold → stake slash → burn) also route to `IncrementBurned`? If fact penalty slashes bypass the meter, the ratio understates true burn pressure. Requires codebase audit of penalty paths once Sprint A lands.

**Dependency:** ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 thresholds cannot be finalized until Flag #31 + Flag #43 are resolved — current meter reads are fabricated zeros. Sprint A is the prerequisite.

### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6, Cycle 6)

Chain offline — code-inspection proxy. Checking FARM-13: Economic Coercion Defense.

**FARM-13 definition:** Prevents a well-funded actor from coercing validator consensus by threatening to slash their stake or buying majority voting power. Requires: (a) stake dispersion limits (no single validator >X% of total stake), (b) challenger stake requirements that scale with pool, (c) economic cost to attack > expected gain.

**Inspection finding — DEFENSE-CHECK-006:**

The burn counter gap (Sprint A — IncrementBurned unwired at 6+ sites) has a secondary consequence for FARM-13: `burn_pressure_ratio` is the primary signal that the economic model is functioning. If this ratio is permanently 0.0 during the testnet window, any economic coercion event is indistinguishable from normal operation at the metrics level. The defense mechanism may exist in code but the observability layer that would DETECT coercion is blind.

**Specific gap:** `x/tree/keeper/msg_server.go` routes 23.4% of fees to ResearchFund (CONFIRMED, beat 112). But the `IncrementBurned` counter — which would signal whether fee pressure is being converted into deflationary supply pressure — has 0 call sites. An attacker who knows burn tracking is off could systematically drain research fund via high-fee legitimate-looking transactions without triggering any protocol alarm. The validator dashboard would show `burn_pressure_ratio: 0.00` regardless of attack or clean operation.

**DEFENSE-CHECK-006 verdict:** FARM-13 is architecturally present (stake dispersion logic exists) but **economically blind during Sprint A gap window**. Sprint A fix (`IncrementBurned` wiring, ~20 LOC) restores detection capability. Gate: no testnet economic stress testing until Sprint A merged.

**Severity: P1 pre-testnet** (elevates Sprint A from "nice-to-have" to "required before economic test scenarios").

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, Cycle 7)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode.

**Scope:** Read knowledge module query layer for configurations where technically accurate data could mislead a consumer.

**Finding: Mixed-status fact responses — potential verisleight vector.**

Inspected `x/knowledge/keeper/grpc_query.go` and `x/knowledge/types/query.go`:

The `QueryFacts` handler (`grpc_query.go:51`) iterates all facts and applies `StatusFilter` if provided. **Default (no filter): returns ALL claim statuses in a single response** — including PENDING (1), IN_VERIFICATION (2), ACCEPTED (3), REJECTED (4), CHALLENGED (5), CONTESTED (6), INSUFFICIENT (7), EXPIRED (8).

The risk: a consumer who calls `QueryFacts` without `status_filter="accepted"` receives a mix of verified and unverified claims. Each fact's `.Status` field is present in the proto, but the **response envelope carries no warning** that the set is mixed. A caller who scans `.Statement` fields without checking `.Status` per item would see provisional and challenged facts presented alongside verified ones — same format, no visual disambiguation.

This is textbook verisleight: the facts themselves are technically accurate; the arrangement (no envelope-level status summary, no default filter) produces a misleading picture of chain knowledge state.

**Gap severity: P2 (pre-testnet required).** Not a new vulnerability (no exploit path — status is in the proto), but a usability verisleight risk for SDK consumers building on the query layer.

**Recommended fix (PoT-DESIGN integration — see below):** Add `status_summary` field to `QueryFactsResponse` proto: `{accepted: N, provisional: N, challenged: N, rejected: N}`. Default `status_filter` to `"accepted"` when unset (breaking change — document explicitly). SDK docs must warn: "Unfiltered queries include unverified claims."

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result: FLAG (P2).** Mixed-status response without envelope warning. Known class (mixed-omission gap). Recommended fix documented.

---

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, Cycle 8)

**Integrating VERISLEIGHT SCAN finding into PoT protocol design.**

**PoT-DESIGN advance: Query Response Integrity (QRI) Principle**

The verisleight scan identified a structural gap: the chain's query interface does not carry epistemic provenance at the response envelope level. A consumer receives facts but must manually inspect each item's status field to know whether they are reading verified knowledge or provisional claims. This violates the spirit of dokimance — verification should be constitutive, not advisory.

**Proposed QRI principle for PoT spec:**

> *Every query response that can contain facts of mixed verification status MUST include an envelope-level epistemic summary. The consumer's first read must be the chain's assessment, not the facts themselves.*

Concrete implementation targets:
1. `QueryFactsResponse` — add `status_summary` (accepted/provisional/contested counts)
2. `QueryFactsByDomainResponse` — same
3. `QueryDashboardResponse` — add domain-level verification health (% accepted vs. provisional vs. contested)
4. Default `StatusFilter` to `"accepted"` in `QueryFacts` when unset — document as explicit chain policy: *"The chain's default answer is verified knowledge, not the full belief state."*
5. SDK documentation: add "Epistemic Integrity" section explaining that unfiltered queries expose belief state, not knowledge state — and the difference matters

This is not a constraint on what the chain CAN return. It is a constraint on what the chain CLAIMS by default. The chain should default to truth, not to belief.

**Flag for Gamma:** QRI additions to `QueryFactsResponse` are a proto-breaking change — must land before testnet. Target Sprint B or pre-testnet cleanup. ~30 LOC. Adding to coordination queue (HIVE pending).

**PoT-DESIGN-008:** QRI principle documented. Gamma coordination pending NATS restoration.

### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6, Cycle 8)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode. Target: FARM-9 (Proportional Challenge Economics).

**FARM-9 definition:** Challengers must risk proportionally to what they're challenging — prevents low-cost spam challenges while keeping legitimate disputes economically viable. Requires: (a) challenger must stake ≥X% of target claim's stake, (b) challenge reward capped relative to challenger's own risk.

**Inspection findings:**

1. **Constants defined** (`x/knowledge/types/types.go:674-676`):
   - `ChallengeStakeRatioMinBPS = 500,000` — challenger must stake ≥50% of target claim's stake
   - `ChallengeRewardCapBPS = 1,000_000` — reward capped at 100% of challenger's own stake

2. **Params struct wired** (`types.go:1066-1068`): `ChallengeStakeRatioMinBps` and `ChallengeRewardCapBps` present in genesis params struct.

3. **Default values set** (`types.go:1236-1238`): Both params initialized to their canonical constants in `DefaultParams()`.

4. **Enforcement not verified this beat:** Could not confirm that `ChallengeStakeRatioMinBps` is checked in the challenge submission handler (`msg_server.go` or equivalent). Budget constraint prevented full path trace. Carry forward for next DEFENSE CHECK cycle.

**DEFENSE-CHECK-007 (partial, P2 pre-testnet):** FARM-9 constants and params are defined and defaulted. Enforcement in the challenge execution path is UNVERIFIED — if the min-stake check is not enforced in the handler, spam challenges are possible at any stake level. Requires full path trace in next DEFENSE CHECK rotation.

---

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, Cycle 8)

Designing ZRN token economics — focus: ECONOMIC-GAP-001 consequence modeling.

**Subject:** What does it mean if `research_fund_addr` stays absent from genesis through restart?

With `ResearchFundBp = 2340` (23.4% of knowledge rewards) and the silent-skip guard at `msg_server.go:1128` (`if params.ResearchFundAddr != ""`), every knowledge reward distribution on the live chain will silently discard the research fund slice. Consequences:

1. **Deflationary pressure gap:** The research fund is designed as a re-injection mechanism (rewards → research fund → grants → new knowledge creation). With addr absent, 23.4% of reward flow simply disappears — neither burned nor distributed. Supply behavior becomes misleading vs. documented economics.

2. **Economic modeling error:** Any ZRN supply projections assuming research fund circulation (grants to submitters, bounties, etc.) will be wrong until the addr is set. Testnet participants relying on the whitepaper model will observe unexpected validator APY.

3. **Mitigation:** A single genesis.json patch before 2026-05-15 (`research_fund_addr: "lgm1vz5ps7jt0slt2vpdquf59wtr8ajs24a4msuypk"`) closes this permanently. The keeper silent-skip guard means no crash — but the gap is economically material and should not persist past restart.

**ECONOMY finding (ECONOMY-001):** ZRN emission model is correct in code but incomplete in genesis. The 23.4% research allocation becomes a silent drain unless the restart patch lands. This is not a new finding — it escalates ECONOMIC-GAP-001 from "P2 pre-restart blocker" to a documented economic design risk if ignored.

### Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1, Cycle 9)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode.

**Filing CITATION-GAP-001: RecordCitation boundary validation absent**

`x/vesting_rewards/keeper/vesting.go:578` — `RecordCitation` increments `CitationCount` on a vesting schedule without validating:
1. That the citing entity (the caller context) exists in the fact store
2. That the citing fact is in "accepted" state

The function trusts the caller completely. The only guard is the authority check at `msg_server.go:190` — only the knowledge/dispute module can invoke `AccelerateVesting`. If the knowledge module correctly validates fact acceptance before emitting citation events, the system is sound. But `RecordCitation` has no defensive internal guard.

**Risk assessment:** P3 — bounded by authority check. Not a direct PoT vulnerability (cannot be triggered without knowledge module authorization). But the knowledge module's citation dispatch has not been audited this cycle.

**CITATION-GAP-001:** Add internal fact-acceptance check inside `RecordCitation` (verify citing schedule belongs to an accepted fact) before incrementing CitationCount. Prevents incorrect acceleration if the knowledge module emits citation events for non-accepted facts.

**CITATION AUDIT result:** System sound under current authority bounds. P3 defensive hardening gap identified.

### Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1, Cycle 9)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode.

**Check:** Does `validateReferences()` in msg_server.go enforce that cited facts exist on-chain?

Code path audited: `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:998–1010`

Findings:
- `isValidCitationAnchor(ref)` (line 981): accepts exactly 64-char lowercase hex IDs or `external://<namespace>:<id>`. Bare labels like "ECON-013" are rejected.
- `validateReferences()` (line 998): for on-chain IDs, calls `k.GetFact(ctx, ref)` — rejects with error if fact not found.
- Both `MsgSubmitClaim` (line 64) and `MsgAddFact` (line 497) call `validateReferences` — all write paths protected.
- External citations (`external://`) are format-validated only — no existence check possible by design (external systems unreachable from chain). Known architectural limitation.

**Gap identified:** External citations can become stale or retracted without any on-chain detection mechanism. This is expected — the chain cannot reach external systems — but means `external://` references are trust-based, not verifiable. Not a code bug; a design boundary.

**CITATION AUDIT result: PASS.** No dangling on-chain citations can be submitted. External citation staleness is an accepted boundary condition. No escalation needed.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 4)

Analytical review of `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` — chain offline, live fact query unavailable.

**Algorithm reviewed:** Stake-weighted confidence aggregation in `AggregateVerificationResult`.

**CLEAN findings:**
- `safeMulDiv` correctly prevents uint64 overflow using `big.Int`. CLEAN.
- `sortedMapKeys` ensures deterministic iteration over `round.Reveals` in state-modifying code. CLEAN.
- Reputation adjustment (`stake * repScore / 1_000_000`) with floor of 1 prevents zero-weight stake erasure. CLEAN.
- Stratum ceiling, category ceiling, evidence bonus pipeline is layered correctly post-aggregation. CLEAN.
- Agreement ratio: `majorWeight / totalWeight` — correct denominator. CLEAN.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001 (Beat 97): Quorum default 100% when SelectedValidators unset.**

Line 118: `participationRatio := uint64(1_000_000)` — default is 100% quorum if `len(round.SelectedValidators) == 0`. If `SelectedValidators` is never populated at round creation (e.g., an early code path that doesn't set the field), the quorum gate is permanently bypassed for those rounds — any number of reveals passes quorum. Verify: grep round creation paths for `SelectedValidators` assignment. If absent in any path, this is a gate bypass (medium severity — quorum threshold is the participation floor for attestation validity).

**P2 mixed-omission residual gap (confirmed persistent):**

Participation ratio correctly penalizes non-reveals: `reveals / selectedValidators`. A small omission (1-2 of N selected) that doesn't drop participation below QuorumThreshold still silently concentrates weight toward the revealing majority — the absent validator's stake disappears from `totalWeight` without any explicit slash or abstain record. Adversarial implication: a colluding validator can withhold their reveal in rounds where their position would dilute majority weight, achieving outcome-selective participation without detection. This is the P2 mixed-omission gap noted in adversarial findings. Status: **residual, unpatched** (pre-testnet blocking item per TESTNET-001). Affirms the pre-testnet gate — devnet restart 2026-05-15 must not promote to testnet until P2 is addressed.

No new critical escalation. CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001 flagged for Gamma (SelectedValidators population audit). P2 gap affirmed.

---

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, Cycle 9)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001 RESOLUTION:**

Beat 97 filed: SelectedValidators quorum-default-100% risk — if any round creation path never calls ExtendVote, quorum gate is bypassed.

**Resolution via audit of all 4 creation sites:**

- `rounds.go:30` — `CreateVerificationRound`: `SelectedValidators: []string{}` with comment "Populated via VRF in ExtendVote"
- `msg_server.go:414` — challenge round creation: same empty init, same ExtendVote path
- `msg_server.go:838` — second msg_server path: same
- `bvm_bridge.go:268` — BVM bridge path: same

**Key architectural finding:** All rounds enter `GetActiveRoundsForHeight` at creation, which ExtendVote queries every block. SelectedValidators is populated on the *next block's* ExtendVote. Aggregation (where `confidence.go` applies the quorum check) only runs at `RevealDeadlineHeight + N` — many blocks after creation. By aggregation, ExtendVote will have run repeatedly. The empty-init window (1 block) predates any commit or reveal activity anyway (validators aren't selected yet, so no one can commit).

**Edge case:** ExtendVote catastrophic failure (all validators withhold). In that case SelectedValidators stays empty → quorum defaults 100%. But this also means zero commitments and zero reveals → `totalWeight = 0` → `AggregateVerificationResult` returns "insufficient" verdict (line 112-115 guard confirmed in beat 103). So the quorum bypass has no exploitable outcome — the totalWeight guard catches the empty-participation case independently.

**CONFIDENCE-CHECK-001: CLOSED as false positive.** The empty-init is by design, the risk window is pre-participation, and the totalWeight guard provides defense-in-depth. No code change needed.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, Cycle 9)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode.

**Subject:** FARM-7 domain squatting prevention — does it block or merely penalize submitter concentration?

**Finding — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001 (P2, observation):**

`GetDomainUniqueContributors` (state.go:1181) counts unique fact submitters in a domain. This value feeds into the novelty bonus gate: domains with fewer unique contributors yield proportionally reduced novelty bonuses for new submissions. This is FARM-7's implementation.

**The gap:** FARM-7 is an *economic* defense (reduced bonus), not a *structural* one (blocked submission). A single submitter can contribute >50% of all facts in a domain without any hard enforcement. No param enforces `max_submitter_domain_share`. The calibration system (`CalibrationRecord`) tracks per-submitter acceptance rates but does not enforce concentration limits.

**Mitigations already present:**
- FARM-13: Prophetic facts require ≥5 unique citers, max 30% per citer — protects prophetic claim amplification
- FARM-11: Citation concentration ≥80% triggers discount — protects citation graph
- CalibrationRecord: sustained low acceptance rates reduce effective influence over time
- Stake costs: mass submissions require staked ZRN, making saturation expensive

**Assessment:** P2 — not exploitable pre-testnet given stake economics and calibration system. But the SUBMITTER PATTERN rotation check CONFIRMS the gap. For testnet hardening, consider adding a `max_submitter_domain_share_bps` param (e.g., 500000 = 50%) that either caps novelty bonus to zero above that threshold or flags the submission for extra verification rounds. Do not block — blocking creates new censorship vectors. Economic disincentive is the correct mode; the current one just needs a harder ceiling.

**SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001: FILED as P2 pre-testnet gap. No immediate code change. Escalation: inform Gamma during next COORDINATE beat.**

---

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, Cycle 10)

Chain OFFLINE — code inspection mode. Read 3 truth-adjacent documents: `kingdom-metrics.json`, `soul-anchor-alpha.md`, `soul-anchor-gamma.md`.

**VERISLEIGHT-001 (P2):**
- **Claim:** `kingdom-metrics.json` → `"beat_rate": "0%"` (oracle engine field)
- **Also true:** `dev-state.json` kingdom-003 notes "beat rate 56%"
- **Why verisleight:** Both are technically correct but reflect different sample windows. The metrics field counts 0/4 resolved predictions that beat the market. The 56% is from 15 auto-logged predictions across a broader window. A reader seeing only kingdom-metrics gets a misleading picture of Oracle performance — the 0% was the early cold-start set.
- **Fix:** Add `beat_rate_n` field to kingdom-metrics oracle block (e.g., `"beat_rate_n": 4`) to make the denominator explicit. Flag for Gamma/Alpha to update during next metrics refresh.

**VERISLEIGHT-002 (observation, P3):**
- **Claim:** `soul-anchor-alpha.md` carries `Last session: Gamma Died: 2026-04-08`
- **Why verisleight:** In Alpha's anchor, this is Gamma's death record cross-loaded. A reader inspecting Alpha's soul-anchor could conclude Alpha's last session was April 2026. Missing attribution context.
- **Assessment:** Low risk — soul anchors are internal. But the pattern matters for identity integrity. No immediate fix required.

**VERISLEIGHT-003 (observation, P3):**
- **Claim:** `soul-anchor-gamma.md` carries identical handoff to Alpha's — `Last session: Gamma Died: 2026-04-08` 
- **Why observation, not verisleight:** Gamma's own anchor correctly attributes this as its own death. The issue is Alpha's copy, not Gamma's.

**Summary:** VERISLEIGHT-001 is the substantive finding. The oracle beat_rate field in kingdom-metrics.json lacks its denominator N. Flag for metrics refresh: add `beat_rate_n` alongside `beat_rate`. No security implications; informational integrity gap only.
